We have a very similar situation this year. Now, a few weeks later, the same economic research institute has announced a new forecast for this year of around 4 percent growth and about 5 percent growth for the next year. These would be figures that are even higher than those of Germany or Switzerland in some forecasts, and that is of course very, very pleasing. There are, of course, various reasons for this. One reason is that the vaccination progress is also one in Austria, which leads to the pandemic being fought step by step and that through our test strategy we have also carried out earlier openings than other countries. This, of course, has brought economic life back faster, and what I believe can also be said is that the rescue measures for businesses and for jobs have also contributed to this, because now there are companies and jobs in companies, including workers. This means that these companies can now quickly take the upswing and thus perhaps come back a little faster than those in other countries. That's very pleasing.

ParlaMint-AT_2021-07-08-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00117_d7e5525	In my view, this is a very sensitive debate. This, of course, has led to a massive increase in government debt while at the same time reducing revenue. This obviously moves some to argue that you should not actually pay attention to how much debt you really have. Only: This is a mistake, of course, because only because everyone has more debt does not mean that debt is no longer a problem at all, it means that the problem as a whole has grown bigger. And you can't press around it. I am rather in favour of looking more closely at the Stability and Growth Pact, which screws must be turned on, so that perhaps the sanctions can also work better. However, I am not in favour of general suspension of debt rules, as some would like, because this would result in a lack of budgetary discipline in many countries. And what happens when the next crisis comes? – Now in times of low interest rates, we can still afford good state financing. This also applies to countries which are highly indebted, such as Italy, Spain, Greece or France. Just what if that's over? What if interest rates rise – in case of high government debt? Then these countries would be and This poses a major problem for the entire euro area. That is why I am still in favour of having to try to reduce debt ratios in economic times.

ParlaMint-AT_2021-07-08-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00117_d7e6777	This is a broad and important discussion that means of production are of course best used where they also produce the highest productivity. We have held a debate on why productivity in many industrialized countries has left behind in recent years. A possible model of explanation is the very, very low interest rates, and this, of course, also means business models which have very low margins. Of course, in the field of economic aid in a crisis, the question arises of how to put it in order to get there, wherever they are really needed. We have done everything we can to ensure that. In the guidelines for all aid, we have put emphasis on the fact that they are granted only to companies that have had a working business model before 31 December 2019 and have not fallen into the insolvency field. This has, I believe, been an adequate restriction, even in the view of the European Union and the Commission. Can it be ruled out in every respect that some company – under quotation marks – has saved “too much”? - No, of course you can't. However, I think that was a consideration on which a good and important decision has been made, because, as I said last year, it is preferable for me to save one or another company, which is perhaps no longer economically viable, too much, than a company that actually has a well-functioning economic model, too little.

ParlaMint-AT_2021-07-08-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00117_d7e6873	Thank you very much for the question. That is also my understanding of what the State should do, and I hope we have done so in an adequate way. The tax receipts were one of the first and very widely accepted aid measures to maintain liquidity in the company. Overall, the amount of support amounted to more than EUR 5 billion, with advance payment reductions as well as general support. We have also designed the rate payment model in such a way that after Covid it is now a very, very generous, long-term one, especially for those companies that have built up more than 50 percent of the residues by Covid. In the normal case, the repayment option is only one year at a 4-percent surcharge on the basic interest rate. We have extended the possibility of repayment for only 2 percent of the basic interest rate to a total of three years. We now also have the first figures on how many companies have claimed this: 335,000 decisions have been sent out and 31,000 companies have taken advantage of this, i.e. a relatively small number, I would say what is very, very pleasing. We see, however, that the volume that these companies have taken is relatively high, over 800 million euros. This means, of course, that in some sectors the flagships also have a big problem – in urban tourism, for example, in congress tourism – and therefore, I think, it is important and good that we have chosen this flexible variant and have also extended the measures in other areas, such as hardening funds or outages bonuses.

ParlaMint-AT_2021-07-08-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00117_d7e9343	Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! Values Advisors! We are debating a number of different legislative proposals here, and I would like to comment briefly on a number of points. First of all, the IFI contribution law: This draft law provides the legal basis for making available the funds for two international financial institutions in the field of multilateral development cooperation, namely, the Asian Development Fund and the International Agricultural Development Fund. In the next ten years, Austria will contribute 18.9 million euros, and the International Agricultural Development Fund will provide 16 million euros for the years 2022 to 2024. Thus, the Austrian share of these two funds remains roughly the same, and we are making a reliable contribution to achieving the UN climate targets in a sustainable way. You know, the development cooperation in Austria is divided into multilateral and bilateral, both are important pillars of international cooperation, the bilateral one certainly has the opportunity to achieve higher direct visibility of Austria in individual projects. On the other hand, it must be said that the multilateral side is, of course, a greater lever by means of the common international challenge and deposit. From the collective law, from TOP 2, I would like to draw up two legislative amendments, namely, the changes relating to the transparency database and, on the other hand, to the Community investment law. It should be said on the transparency database that, as mentioned by my previous speaker, Austria will receive money from the European Union's reconstruction fund. It is not yet quite clear how much exactly it depends a little on the growth rates this year, but there will probably be around 3.5 billion euros. It is clear that transparency must be ensured for the use of the funds. All benefits financed through the reconstruction fund are therefore also entered in the transparency database. In order to ensure that the relevant authorities have access and can pass on the data where it is permitted and necessary, it is necessary to amend the Federal Tax Code in order to enable the tax authority to understand the data contained in the transparency database. It is possible to There has already been a lot of talk about developing the transparency database step by step. I am perfectly right, Mrs Colleague, that we have not yet reached the target. This Commission report has had some terrifying results, and that is why all the parties here in Parliament – and that is something I have seen as very positive – have agreed that we are together considering how we can completely recast the BAT. In my view, these negotiations – and I said this yesterday in the Committee on the Rules of Procedure – are a real positive example of how living parliamentaryism can work, how we can work together on legislative projects, how we can discuss different points of view, which there is no doubt, together, with an attempt to find the best possible solution for all parties and, above all, for the state of Austria. We want – and that is, I believe, the concern of all here in Parliament – that constitutional protection, the protection of the state of his work can be carried out and that he can do this well. But what we would like and what is not sufficiently guaranteed in our view is that it can also be controlled in a comprehensive and accompanying manner by Parliament. There are really massive improvements in the reform inside, which is something we see in any case, but from our point of view, this comprehensive, genuine control by Parliament is not foreseen. Secret services, intelligence services, authorities such as the BAT operate in the dark. They have to do that, otherwise they can't do their job. However, if an authority is acting in the dark, it is all the more necessary to have a strong Parliament with the greatest possible means of control, because only in such a way as to ensure that the constitutional protection, which unfortunately has been a pretty black box for years, in several ways, is controlled accordingly. The control committee, which is now being introduced, is far better than the status quo, and it also gives the opportunity to control accordingly at the request of the relevant committee in Parliament. But as we have already said, this is not far enough. I am convinced that the Commission's control powers must go further and not only be able to refer to completed investigations. That can't be enough from my point of view. I am also fully aware that it would be quite complex to find a solution, as can be checked by the way in ongoing investigations; but I am also convinced that we would have had to face this challenge even more and found a solution. Consider the example of the terrorist attack of the 2nd century. November: A control committee could not act because the investigations have not yet been completed. I am convinced that a control taking place during the investigations is positive. There are such examples that you only need to look at them in Germany. There are special individual cases of political debate which are the subject of public reporting, the possibility that Parliament, the control bodies, will also be able to carry out monitoring. This is not the case here, so there is no agreement from us NEOS either. Nevertheless, as I said earlier, I consider the way in which we have worked together as a Parliament here to bring meaningful different points of view to a common reform as something very special. Unfortunately, this is not always the case in the National Council or in the Parliament, and I believe that we should take this as a positive example and see that we discuss different positions better and perhaps come to a overall better solution for citizens in this country.

Ladies and gentlemen, the content only briefly – I don't want to repeat it all –: Recasting of the BAT, now under the name of the Department of State Protection and Intelligence, in short DSN – it will take a little while for us to have the accelerator all intus – with the Terminus Constitutional Protection as a super term. It is also important to us that, compared to the first version of the government template, it has succeeded in developing this separation even more. This is very important, and it is perhaps also important to explain this to the audience: The people who work in the intelligence service are then no longer police officers. This is important because there are always conflicts of interest. This was in fact also a bit of the birth defects of the old BAT that the tasks of the intelligence service and the police tasks were so closely linked. So that's an important story. There is an attempt, in my view, to depoliticize the leadership. As an Austrian learnt, of course, it will never be complete in Austria – it does not have to be, because it cannot be that once you have been politically active anywhere, you can't become anything at all in a top position at all. But there is a cooling phase, and there is a serious attempt to depoliticize the management positions. There is a strengthening of the secondary employment arrangements – which was particularly important to us as a Freedom Party, which has also demonstrated the experience of the past – and a much improved internal control of the activity of constitutional protection through the mandatory introduction of appropriate quality assurance systems. Case-conferences on the State Protection tasks will be introduced in accordance with the security police case-conferences. This is essentially a matter of ensuring that measures and information from different actors to prevent constitutional attacks are coordinated as efficiently as possible. It is also a lesson from the events in the run-up to the terrorist attack that information that is available is also used accordingly. There is a massive strengthening in the intelligence service in the areas of analysis and information collection, and, now we come to the crucial point for our approval, that is the expansion of parliamentary control – there is now new, the reporting obligation of the Federal Minister to the Parliament, concrete to the Standing Subcommittee of the Internal Affairs Committee, the so-called Secret Service Committee. This has not been the case so far, since we have always had to put things out of the nose to the Minister, and now there is this reporting obligation, in particular on substantial changes to the situations relating to internal security. Furthermore, the independent and non-disputed control committee, which has already been referred to several times, is being set up to monitor the activity of the entire constitutional protection. The members of this control committee are elected here in the House by a majority of two-thirds. This is a constitutional provision, That is, a future government cannot simply change it for its benefit. As far as the Rules of Procedure are concerned, in my view, it is a really big throw, which together has succeeded in bringing about the implementation of minority law. That is to say, we liberals, together with SPÖ and NEOS, could instruct this control committee to examine certain issues. It's a little too bad that NEOS are not there. I must say, however, that the NEOS have provided valuable inputs, particularly in connection with our original joint opposition request concerning Rules of Procedure. You can always have more, but it's clear: In a negotiation you never get 120 percent, in fact not 100 percent, but you have to see that you negotiate as much as possible. I think that has been successful, and that is precisely what the NEOS is about, namely the control during ongoing investigation procedures, is a very sensitive issue. I'm skeptical, too. I believe that we are Members and not investigators here, and that is why this is basically all right. In my opinion, parliamentary control is now really a milestone in this complex. Furthermore, there is – you must not forget – also an evaluation after at the latest five years, in addition to quality assurance. That means that this is a ongoing process, it will surely be recognized there or there need for improvement, and that can be repaired accordingly. Of course, we will also make use of our options for minority rights accordingly. Ladies and gentlemen! We make good decisions today. This is good and important, on the one hand, for the people who then work in this new authority, but this is particularly important for the trust of international partner services and the trust of citizens in that authority responsible for public security in that country and making a great contribution to the country's internal security. – I thank everyone involved.

ParlaMint-AT_2021-07-08-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00117_d7e12746	Mr President. Estimated Minister! Ladies and gentlemen. Dear ladies and gentlemen in front of the screens and on the gallery! In principle, the present bill has a number of good intentions. However, the bill does not fulfil the claim it has given itself, because it is still really immature in some places and unfortunately continues the social selection at universities. We cannot accept this law in the present form, for which I would like to raise three points: This draft seeks to create an attractive and also financially interesting training offer for universities. He does not answer, however, how to prevent the offer for working students in regular and free studies from being diluted in the future and the students who work are increasingly forced into payable courses. A largely free and high-quality study must continue to be in fact ensured for all, including for professionals and for persons with alternative university access rights, such as apprentices or skilled workers. These possibilities of study must also be widely informed so that people, if they are interested in these things, also recognize themselves. Secondly, the law only provides for a subsequent quality examination of the courses, even if there are reasonable doubts. I do not think this is acceptable, because the students who have registered for the courses have to pay a lot of money, invest precious life, usually complete these courses in a professional way and do not know whether the quality fits and whether the acceptance of the diplomas is also available at the end of the day. I think this is a serious disadvantage in this template that needs to be corrected. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] The present legislative proposal also provides for the continuation of social selective access restrictions by 2027. The recent evaluation of the IHS has shown how socially selective access restrictions work: In the academic year 2019/20, the proportion of persons whose parents do not have a university degree decreased on average over all subjects, from 53 to 51 percent of those enrolled. The selection in human medicine is particularly large, where the proportion falls by 10 percent, but also at the Business and Economics degree at the University of Economics in Vienna, where 28 applicants whose parents do not have a degree in higher education were finally able to write only 22. I don't think that's justified, that's not equal treatment. In view of these figures, the free services provided by the law for admission procedures in medical and psychological subjects are not sufficient, but they should be set up widely for all. If the access restrictions are to be maintained by 2027, the support offers must also be available throughout the period, for all subjects. That is why the law as a whole does not seem to me mature and we cannot agree. But we would ask you to think about the corrections and perhaps intensify them, so that it really becomes a fair law for all students, whether working or not. I would like to suggest that, as we have arrived in an international world, we are finally taking care of the mutual recognition of the diplomas in the EU, because this is still not regulated and nostrification procedures are quite difficult. Apprentices are better advised because there are at least agreements for a number of apprenticeships that are mutually charged in the EU countries. In the final effect, it also has an impact on the payment in that country in which you work, and that is not justified at a time like today. Thank you.

[[Acceptance with the ÖVP and the Abg. Maurer.]] I now come to answer the questions. There are 93 questions that have been sent to us by the Austrian Freedom Party at 10 a.m. I express my thanks to the officials of the Ministry of the Interior for the prompt and comprehensive response. Question 1: In the last one and a half years, we have represented a consistent and clear course against illegal migration. On the one hand, the BMI has focused on zero tolerance in criminal asylum seekers and protectionists. Top priority is to conclude the procedure more quickly and to initiate decommissioning procedures on the basis of criminal permissibility, including the extradition of criminal offences. In addition, we introduced 72-hour rapid procedures in mid-2020, by the way, a measure that I have missed in the time of the FPÖ Minister of the Interior Herbert Kickl and which we have now used to ensure that people who will not have residence rights in Austria and also within the European Union will receive their first-instance notice within 72 hours. We have put in place a number of measures to strengthen border protection at all levels. In March 2020, Austrian police officers supported the Greek border police in the field of external border protection. We have used several bilateral police quotas in the Balkans to better secure borders, and we have been able to upgrade our own border protection through innovative projects such as state-of-the-art drone technology. On question 2: Austria is acting self-confidently and with clear positions in the EU and in the European bodies – that is now the detailed answer to the question of FPÖ, what we are doing at international level – we were able to set up the platform against illegal migration last year with the support of Horst Seehofer and representatives of many other European countries. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Deimek.]] It has its headquarters in Vienna and coordinates operational measures by European countries together with the Western Balkans. There are four main topics: Border protection, tow control, faster procedures and returns. In the course of the work on the platform, we have already been able to work together with some Balkan countries on a comprehensive return plan. Our aim is to start repatriation before the gates of the European Union, as I described earlier, and to bring people who are not likely to stay, for example, from Pakistan, directly back to their homes from the Balkan countries. In addition, I pursue a clear policy of the three safety networks covering the entire migration route. Firstly, a strong external migration policy with the three objectives: Strengthen protection and prospects on the ground, prevent illegal migration and ensure return. To this end, I have launched numerous new projects and measures in third countries, such as border protection in Tunisia, so that Tunisia recognises that we are negotiating with it as Austria at an eye level, and that repatriations to Tunisia are later possible. Returns are always possible – this is also a rule of law, even if the former Minister of the Interior should know that – if the country of origin issues so-called home travel certificates. At the EU's common border, we support our partners, such as Greece, but also the countries on the Western Balkans, in order to prevent illegal entry. Within the EU and Austria, I advocate an effective and sustainable reform of the common European asylum system and of Schengen to prevent illegal migration to Europe, safeguard external borders and prevent secondary migration within the EU. Our goal as an EU must be to get to grips quickly and on the issues we agree on, because there are three specific areas, as I mentioned earlier, in which I believe that this should be possible quickly: consistent repatriation, stronger external border protection and faster asylum procedures. However, I clearly reject a mandatory distribution within the European Union. As I explained earlier: Austria is one of the most taxed countries in the European Union, we are in third, 24 EU Member States offering less protection than Austria. Question 3: The proposals shall be made regularly in all political bodies, in particular the Council of Ministers of the Interior, and at all expert levels. Question 4: Austria can count on strong networks of partner countries in achieving its respective objectives and use them on the subject. Cooperation with countries such as Denmark, the Salzburg Forum partners and the respective current and future Council Presidents, most recently Germany and now Slovenia, is particularly close. Question 5: Good progress has been made on Eurodac Regulation and Screening Regulation. In addition, priorities, such as the mandatory border procedure at the EU's external borders, could be set. Many Member States also share my focus on the external dimension as a key to a sustainable asylum and migration policy, as well as the current conclusions of the European Council. On question 6: I am constantly in close contact with the relevant members of the EU Commission – Ylva Johansson, EU Vice-President of the Schinas Commission and Commissioner Várhelyi – in order to clarify Austrian positions. We have also found strong partners for the Austrian position in Commissioner Várhelyi and EU Vice-President Schinas. Question 7: The Commission's proposals as a basis for discussion are a step in the right direction, but I do not go far enough in many areas. Positive are, for example, the new visa lever and, for the first time, partially mandatory asylum and return border procedures. In part, the package of the EU Commission also focuses on outdated concepts such as mandatory distribution, which from our point of view have failed clearly and cannot lead to a future solution. Since a sustainable solution to asylum and migration policy is only possible jointly at EU level, I will continue to work constructively on the reform of the system. Question 8: Specific examples of need for improvement are: mandatory – but flexible – solidarity of EU Member States in order to avoid this pointless discussion of the EU distribution; strengthening the scope of mandatory border procedures; consistent handling of criminal offences; enhanced measures to prevent genuine secondary migration; rapid and efficient asylum return procedures in all EU Member States; new partnerships with third countries in order to prevent illegal migration already in regions of origin and transit; Strengthen protection capacities in the region of origin and ensure return. Questions 9 to 11: We are involved in bilateral talks, consultations and negotiations with the Ministry of Foreign Affairs on readmission agreements or alternative readmission agreements with third countries such as Afghanistan, Morocco, Iran, India, Mongolia, Kazakhstan and similar countries. As it is not formally permitted for an EU Member State to enter into bilateral negotiations or to conclude readmission agreements as soon as the EU Commission has a corresponding negotiating mandate, various efforts are being made to improve return cooperation with third countries, but the European Commission should continue to be obliged to use its negotiating power of a region of around 500 million people for effective return cooperation. Questions 12 and 13: The increase in return rates and the improvement of cooperation with countries of origin are one of my declared priorities at European level. I have therefore also been particularly committed to the application of the visa lever in the Visa Code. This provides for higher barriers to visa allocation for nationals of States who do not cooperate sufficiently with the EU Member States in the field of readmission. The European Commission now has a wide range of instruments from formal readmission agreements to alternative agreements and, in addition to important third countries, broad, comprehensive so-called migration dialogues are also being carried out. Questions 14 to 16: There is neither a list of safe third countries at European or national level. In addition, at European level, Austria is committed to a list of safe third countries in the future, and the concept of safe third countries is applied on the basis of a case-by-case examination for all asylum seekers. Questions 17 to 20: The willingness of third countries to cooperate will be subject to ongoing national and European evaluation, and the so-called visa lever has been established as a legal instrument for assessing return cooperation. The purpose of the amendment is to use visa policy as a lever to improve cooperation in readmission and thus to reduce the risk of illegal migration. Since then, a regular qualitative assessment of a third country's willingness to cooperate has been carried out in the European Commission's readmission on the basis of various criteria. This approach ensures a pan-European approach towards third countries that do not cooperate adequately with EU Member States. On questions 21 to 23: Yes, there is the agreement on work. Due to the Covid-19 Pandemic and its factual limitations, the clarification of essential details on the implementation of the work agreement is still pending. ParlaMint-AT_2021-07-08-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00117_d7e20945	Madam President. Dear ladies and gentlemen! I think it makes sense for us to consider the question of dealing with the world heritage sites in Austria as well. However, I would like to say: The world heritage does not only include culture and monuments. The world heritage includes nature, nature reserves, and the world heritage of Unesco also includes immaterial elements such as the Italian pizza. The Italian pizza is a World Heritage Site. But how is a monument supposed to deal with pizza? – This is neither the officials, nor anything else. The pizza or perhaps the Wiener Apfelstrudel, the Wiener Schnitzel, the Wiener Fiaker – I am now a bit wonned –: I think the Austrian Monument Office will be hard to deal with these issues. We tend to become a world heritage law in Austria, because the protection of monuments as a very object-oriented one – in terms of monuments, architectural objects – is simply overwhelmed in this respect, alone with the ten world heritage sites in Austria. To be mentioned are the Neusiedlersee and the landscape zone around it – I deliberately say landscape zone – the beech forests of the Carpathians that are already beginning in Austria, and also the Wachau. The Wachau is, of course, primarily a landscape, although also a cultural landscape. Whether the stakes of the Stone Age in Austria are really considered by the monument protection? – These are of course also connected to the lake in which they are located, they are ensembles. This brings us to the topic of ensemble protection. Ensemble protection is not primarily a concern of the Austrian Monument Protection Office and the relevant laws. It's an object protection. In other words, ensemble protection is of course a very strong task for the communities, and we are therefore with the authorities and also the political institutions on the ground, the local authorities. This is already a matter of finding solutions that are practical for local citizens. It is also about urban development, the development of the cities, and therefore the problem of visual axes, on the the Unesco value, also a municipal problem, because urban development should not be restricted. In Austria: Graz has a problem with the protection of the roof landscape. De facto roof constructions are not allowed at all. Innsbruck has not even sought the World Heritage Site, and Paris should have given up the wonderful glass pyramid in the Louvre. Paris has intelligently protected only the banks of the city river, but not the entire city center. In conclusion, therefore, an Austrian World Heritage Act should also be federalistic and subsidiary. That is to say, the regional, the local level must be able to speak with a strong voice. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2021-07-08-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00117_d7e21232	Tradition is not the worship of ashes, but the passing on of fire, should Gustav Mahler have once said. The same applies to the world heritage. Madam President. Mrs. State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! Values Seers at home! The cultural and natural heritage is one of the inestimable and irreplaceable goods not only of every people but of all humanity. The Unesco World Heritage Site, which protects world heritage sites, is an international agreement between currently 193 Contracting States, which has existed since 1972. Austria signed it in 1992. The World Heritage Convention has a simple legal status in Austria – that has already been mentioned by colleague Blimlinger – and is immediately applicable without being transformed into national law. In our country there are a total of ten sites of the World Heritage Site, including the historic centre of Graz, also the capital of my home country. By signing, Austria undertook to protect and preserve the cultural and natural heritage sites within its borders with exceptional universal value and to provide international assistance and support under the Convention. In February 2019, the International Council for Monumental Care presented a test report, the so-called Heritage Impact Assessment. It sets out clear recommendations on what the Republic should do to preserve the status of world heritage in the long term. As foreseen in the government programme, improvements are now to be made in two respects: on the one hand, the mention of the Unesco World Heritage as part of public interest in the Austrian Monument Protection Act, on the other, the addition of the Monument Protection Act with regard to the active application of ensemble protection, the protection of culturally important visual axes and the maintenance of monuments. This points to the importance of existing and future world heritage sites and sends a clear signal on the part of the federal government. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] As a teacher and a cultural interested person, the education of our children in the field of art and culture is also a matter of my heart. It is important to me that our students for art and culture become enthusiastic and that the cultural heritage is brought closer to them. I know that our teachers also do a lot in this field – a warm thank you for that. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and by Mr Bürstmayr and Mr Disoski.]] At this point I would like to wish all students – I hope you will allow me – and also all teachers with their school boards, who have done great things this school year, a beautiful and relaxing summer. Ladies and gentlemen, Austria is a great cultural nation and we can be proud of it. We have great artists in our country. ParlaMint-AT_2021-07-08-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00117_d7e21420	Next speaker: Mrs Astrid Rössler. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2021-07-08-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00117_d7e21441	Madam President. Thank you, Mrs. Federal Minister! Thank you very much, Mr State Secretary! As has already been pointed out, the current amendment to the Aviation Act contains a number of technical adaptations, implementation of Union law, but also some very interesting and important changes in the content which I would like to address briefly. On the one hand, we are dealing with the obstacles to air transport. A register will be set up, which will also be accessible accordingly. However, there is an important change in the security zone: Many know that the security zones around airports are quite significant and also different from settlement areas, and there is an important change that photovoltaic systems and solar installations, which have been subject to approval or which have also existed with certain ambiguities, have now been granted up to 100 square meters in size, i.e. in the future, there will be no further authorisation under the Aviation Act – a part to achieve that we cannot be inhibited by additional authorisation procedures in the development of renewable energies. The second, important part also concerns pre-eminent rights and communication with neighbouring people. There are often disputes about possible noise pollution, now this law sets out that all airports must carry out at least a minimum level of noise measurements – that is a contract to airport holders – and these measurements must also be published accordingly. A number of airports in Austria have already done this, but it is established that it is mandatory for all airports; it is simply for disclosure and clarification. From my point of view, the publication of flight tracks is also a step which will come and also serves clarity. The third part, which I consider very significant and important from the point of view of the possible predicaments or concerns, is the civil aviation events. Up to now, these events have been subject to approval only from the focus of public safety or aviation safety, and it is now accepted that authorisation can be explicitly refused even if this entails disproportionate noise exposure to the population concerned. There is Indeed, numerous complaints that often events without the involvement of the region lead to a considerable noise pollution. In the future, this can be completely outlawed for the first time for reasons of noise pollution – a very important improvement. That is why I am seeking broad approval of this aviation law novel.

ParlaMint-AT_2021-07-08-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00117_d7e24211	Mrs Bundesminister Geessler has spoken on a point of view. - Please, Mrs. Federal Minister.
ParlaMint-AT_2021-07-08-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00117_d7e24232	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! Allow me first to refer to the agenda items. In fact, we are discussing agenda items 17 and 18, two novels that are on the agenda with good reason today, so I would like to briefly explain what you are voting on today. Novelle to the Bundesstraßen-Mautgesetz: This is about implementing an EU directive on the interoperability of electronic toll systems and facilitating cross-border information exchange. This will allow the cross-border exchange of data from authorised owners for whose vehicles the incorrect payment of the toll on the Asfinag toll network has been established. This means that we expect a better traceability of administrative penalties for the incorrect payment of the toll on the Asfinag toll network. It is an adaptation to an EU legislation, but I believe it is an important one. The Novelle to the Federal Road Law, TOP 18: This too is a multiplicity of novelties, I would like to say. On the one hand, it is about connecting park-and-ride facilities to federal roads, improving interoperability. We've had this before. Mobility is about the right mix. With the improvement of the park-and-ride and park-and-drive systems, with the clarifications in this novel, we take an important step. We also have the implementation of the directive on the control of the dangers of serious accidents within it, as well as an improvement in the federal planning competence regarding federal roads. This concerns areas to ensure the implementation of expansion projects and of necessary facilities, which can be explained by a regulation of the BMK more easily to the Federal Road Planning Area. The novelle contains many other points, including a point that was always very important to Tyrol and which there have been questions from different federal states, in terms of connection points and connections – that is, an extensive and diverse novelle. I also hope for broad approval of this amendment. [[Congratulations with the Greens and representatives of the ÖVP.]] To request, I can only repeat what I have said the last few days. I think we have taken on a great responsibility, all of us together. We have taken on great responsibility for the climate, for nature conservation, for the future of our children. We have set common goals and we also demand them regularly. In infrastructure projects, that is perfectly clear, many interests sit at the table. There is, of course, road safety on the table. That's where the interest of the federal states is at the table. That's where the business interests sit at the table. There are many different interests at the table. With me, climate protection is also at the table. That is why we will now evaluate this construction program by autumn. In autumn there will also be a result, which will then be the basis for further decisions. In the meantime, I have received an extensive parliamentary request from Mr Ottenschlager and will, of course, answer it accordingly. But this is a good, important access right now, and as has been said, the result will be in autumn. Until then, I hope to have an objective discussion on this subject. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-AT_2021-07-08-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00117_d7e26815	Mr Zorba is speaking on behalf of the Committee on the Environment, Public Health and Consumer Protection. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2021-07-08-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00117_d7e26837	Mr President. Mrs. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Appreciate Seers and Advisors! As it seems, there is broad consensus on this item on the agenda. This consensus also exists in the Federal Government when it comes to exploiting the opportunities of digitization and making management efficient. [[Congratulation with Greens and ÖVP.]] What's this about? – This novel is intended to implement the once-only principle. This project is essential for simple data collection and efficient administrative data exchange and, from my point of view, is a milestone for red tape reduction. In this context, Once only means that data only have to be reported and collected once in the performance of information obligations to the authorities. In order to achieve this, the federal corporate service portal will be further developed into a single digital access gate, the register and system network. This registry and system network can then be used to exchange internal data both nationally and across the EU. Since the company service portal already has a high interface density to other public administration systems, it is particularly suitable as a basis for this project. For data protection, to colleagues Köllner: In creating such a system, of course, data protection was of particular concern to us. This novelle does not explicitly issue a general authorisation for the cross-border exchange of data, but rather creates the technical and organisational basis for efficient transmission. [[Congratulations with the Greens and representatives of the ÖVP.]] Accordingly, it was also clarified in the legislative text that data transmission is only permitted if there is a sufficient data protection basis for this. What does it bring? – Colleague Himmelbauer has already explained it in detail. Entrepreneurs know that, they always have to collect and enter the same data, and that will be easier in the future. This saves time and money in the future. In the future, companies will be able to take basic steps digitally at a central point as well as make other registrations for administrative procedures in accordance with the Once-only principle, such as the registration for financial online or social security services. This also implements essential demands of Mr Hoyos's motion for a resolution, such as the establishment of a central application and resolution body for the establishment of a company. This discharge is particularly beneficial for companies in their founding phase or for start-ups. The pandemic has shown us how important it is to take further steps in the field of digitalisation in order to make daily work easier for businesses, authorities and the population. [[Congratulation with Greens and ÖVP.]] In this way we contribute further to the crisis strength of administration and economy and enable greater efficiency through red tape reduction. Thank you.

The Action Plan on Sustainable Procurement focuses primarily on the new standards – from office materials to appliances to textiles – certifications that are necessary there, but also on the public hand as a large lever, as an important client when it comes to certifying events as green events and thus anchoring minimum standards – climate-friendly standards, environmental standards – with durability, spare parts, reparability as criteria. There is a very, very service-oriented website and a very dedicated sustainability team, the sustainable procurement of the public hand currently at federal level, but hopefully soon also at national, urban and municipal level declared binding, and the BBG, which also serves and advises with a very dedicated sustainability team. This is a much more concrete approach that combines quality, domestic production, short ways and sustainability criteria. Therefore, the sustainability procurement plan is currently the best answer for our domestic businesses.

We discussed and decided yesterday the EAG, the Renewable Development Act, and yes, there was a lot of good, and therefore NEOS also contributed to it. But one point – I said it yesterday – has unfortunately been forgotten or not negotiated, and that is this Earth wiring of 110 kV lines, which has failed once again on the resistance of the ÖVP. Unfortunately, you have to say: The Greens could not prevail, and the SPÖ, which was really involved in the negotiations, did not have a very high priority list either. That's very bad. Why is it a pity?– It is not a side-by-theme, it is not a side-site, how often it is discussed, because the energy transition will only work if there is the corresponding pipeline infrastructure. It will be necessary to create the energy transition, and it will only be possible to create it if one takes away the citizens and takes this issue very seriously, especially, of Earth cabling instead of freeing. What do we have to do? – A uniform framework across the country needs to be established. There is a need for a uniform framework for planning security and legal certainty, otherwise we will simply not be able to do so. I have already said that otherwise, by 2030, we will simply not create the energy transition with the necessary renewable plants. I want to make it clear again, because it always comes back: Putting 110 kV lines under the ground is really not an experimental technology. That may have been it once, 30, 40 years ago, but it's not been a long time. It is an international standard that is really being made everywhere outside of Austria. In Austria, it is precisely because the ÖVP does not go along with it, and especially because the energy suppliers are against it. What then are the topics that come like this? What is the theme that comes from the ÖVP? – This is the case with the costs. That's what they say: Well, that's a lot, much more expensive, and that would turn on the customer. – But that, ladies and gentlemen, is nonsense, it is really nonsense! There are automated ploughs that plow these Earth cables under the ground, which is fully automated, and that is in the core no more expensive than a free line. These costs are not yet included in the external factors, so that a property loses value if such a free line stands, which is not yet within this bill. What is not inside is the damage to natural disasters. It is just a few weeks ago that we had this terrible storm, this Tornado in the Czech Republic, on the Austrian border, through which, among other things, a lot of free lines have been destroyed – I think you have all the pictures in your head as well. The second argument – if you don't continue with the costs – is: Well, that's not happening in Austria because it's a network deleted, and the cable reserve, that's only limited valid. There are, of course, also very, very many opinions, international opinions from experts that these factors would be very cost-effective to solve with the right transformer infrastructures. So that's no longer the big problem. What was, of course, annoyed yesterday – and now I come back to the EAG – is that 380 kV lines have been taken into account in the EAG. Now you need to know: The 380 kV line is technologically much more demanding, but it stands inside. The 110 kV line, which truly meets international standards, has failed to reach the EAG. That's why you're wondering, of course. Yes, and then you have to know that the need for 380 kV lines in Austria will not be very high for the next few years and decades, you probably don't need one. 110 kV lines will, in contrast, require very, very many. Of course, one has to say that in this context one has seen very clearly where the power sits. The power sits in the countries that sit with the national leaders, who sit with the black state princes and their energy suppliers, and the Federal Government once again has not enforced itself – and that is wrong, ladies and gentlemen. That is why I would like to create a uniform federal framework for modern energy infrastructure in the interests of the population, instead of this federal Fleckerltepich, which we just have and which we seem to have no other way to handle; and that is my request for it. I would like to say now that I am not pleased that I have to bring it in again, because – once again – I think we had a great opportunity yesterday to solve it, but if it is, it is. I take the next step with this request and look forward to the debate in autumn. Thank you.
